# milestone11

## index

- 001 — builtins: typeof without an argument
- 002 — builtins: sizeof with two arguments
- 003 — builtin: typeof on a value, a string and a struct
- 004 — builtin: typeof on a ref and on a type name
- 005 — builtin: sizeof folds to a constant
- 006 — builtin: typeof / sizeof on scalars
- 007 — builtin: typeof / sizeof on an array
- 008 — builtin: sizeof without parentheses
- 009 — builtin: typeof without parentheses
- 010 — builtin: typeof and sizeof on chars, bool, and a literal
- 011 — builtin: sizeof of every scalar type
- 012 — builtin: sizeof of a struct honours ABI padding
- 013 — builtin: typeof of a struct gives its name
- 014 — cast: as casting + i64 / i16 via explicit cast
- 015 — cast: as converts between f32 and the integer types
- 016 — cast: char arithmetic and an expression cast back to char
- 017 — cast: a struct cannot be cast to or from a scalar
- 018 — cast: casting a scalar to a struct
- 019 — literal: f32 literals: declare, print, zero-init, in a function
- 020 — literal: f32 arithmetic + comparison, compound assignment
- 021 — literal: f64 declaration, arithmetic and compound assignment
- 022 — literal: a f32 literal takes the type of its target
- 023 — literal: casts between f64, f32 and the integer types
- 024 — literal: a return that does not match the function's type
- 025 — literal: integer literals at the edge of each type's range
- 026 — literal: an integer literal too wide for i32 keeps its width
- 027 — literal: a literal that does not fit its target type
- 028 — literal: a negative literal at the low edge still fits
- 029 — operator: bitwise & | ^ << >>
- 030 — operator: unary not / minus / bitwise-not
- 031 — operator: compound bitwise assignment
- 032 — operator: bitwise operator on f32 operands is rejected
- 033 — operator: divide by zero prints file:line and exits 1
- 034 — operator: f32 division by zero traps (not inf)
- 035 — literal: f32 and f64 math, casts and printf

---

## 001 — builtins: typeof without an argument

```ura
// error: 'typeof' takes one argument, got 0
fn main() i32:
   x i32 = 1
   return typeof()
```

### stderr

```
error: test.ura:4:11 'typeof' takes one argument, got 0
  |
4 |    return typeof()
  |           ^^^^^^
help: use typeof(x)
error: test.ura:4:11 expected 'i32', found 'i8[]'
  |
4 |    return typeof()
  |           ^^^^^^
error: aborting due to 2 errors
```

### status

```
1
```

---

## 002 — builtins: sizeof with two arguments

```ura
// error: 'sizeof' takes one argument, got 2
fn main() i32:
   x i32 = 1
   return sizeof(x, x)
```

### stderr

```
error: test.ura:4:11 'sizeof' takes one argument, got 2
  |
4 |    return sizeof(x, x)
  |           ^^^^^^
help: use sizeof(x)
error: aborting due to 1 error
```

### status

```
1
```

---

## 003 — builtin: typeof on a value, a string and a struct

```ura
struct User:
   count i32

fn main() i32:
   x i32 = 1
   s i8[] = "abc"
   u User
   io::out($"{typeof(x)} {typeof(s)} {typeof(u)}\n")
   return 0
```

```llvm
; ModuleID = 'ura'
source_filename = "ura"
target datalayout = "e-m:e-p270:32:32-p271:32:32-p272:64:64-i64:64-f80:128-n8:16:32:64-S128"
target triple = "x86_64-pc-linux-gnu"

%String = type { i8*, i32, i32 }
%User = type { i32 }

@TYPEOF = private unnamed_addr constant [4 x i8] c"i32\00", align 1
@TYPEOF.1 = private unnamed_addr constant [5 x i8] c"i8[]\00", align 1
@TYPEOF.2 = private unnamed_addr constant [5 x i8] c"User\00", align 1
@str = private unnamed_addr constant [10 x i8] c"%s %s %s\0A\00", align 1

define i8* @"new<i8>"(i32 %0) {
entry:
  %count = alloca i32, align 4
  store i32 %0, i32* %count, align 4
  %"$count" = load i32, i32* %count, align 4
  %MUL = mul i32 %"$count", ptrtoint (i8* getelementptr (i8, i8* null, i32 1) to i32)
  %AS = sext i32 %MUL to i64
  %call.calloc = call i8* @calloc(i64 %AS, i64 sext (i32 ptrtoint (i8* getelementptr (i8, i8* null, i32 1) to i32) to i64))
  ret i8* %call.calloc
}

define void @"copy<i8>"(i8* %0, i8* %1, i32 %2) {
entry:
  %dst = alloca i8*, align 8
  %src = alloca i8*, align 8
  %count = alloca i32, align 4
  store i8* %0, i8** %dst, align 8
  store i8* %1, i8** %src, align 8
  store i32 %2, i32* %count, align 4
  %"$dst" = load i8*, i8** %dst, align 8
  %"$src" = load i8*, i8** %src, align 8
  %"$count" = load i32, i32* %count, align 4
  %MUL = mul i32 %"$count", ptrtoint (i8* getelementptr (i8, i8* null, i32 1) to i32)
  %AS = sext i32 %MUL to i64
  %MUL1 = mul i64 %AS, sext (i32 ptrtoint (i8* getelementptr (i8, i8* null, i32 1) to i32) to i64)
  %call.memcpy = call i8* @memcpy(i8* %"$dst", i8* %"$src", i64 %MUL1)
  ret void
}

define void @"free<i8>"(i8* %0) {

  %data = alloca i8*, align 8
  store i8* %0, i8** %data, align 8

declare i64 @write(i32, i8*, i64)

define void @"io::out"(%String %0) {
entry:
  %text = alloca %String, align 8
  store %String %0, %String* %text, align 8
  %call.String.chars = call i8* @String.chars(%String* %text)
  %call.String.len = call i32 @String.len(%String* %text)
  %AS = sext i32 %call.String.len to i64
  %call.write = call i64 @write(i32 1, i8* %call.String.chars, i64 %AS)
  call void @String.drop(%String* %text)
  ret void
}

define i32 @main() {
entry:
  %x = alloca i32, align 4
  %s = alloca i8*, align 8
  %arr = alloca [4 x i8], align 1
  %u = alloca %User, align 8
  %format.size = alloca i32, align 4
  %format.text = alloca i8*, align 8
  store i32 1, i32* %x, align 4
  store [4 x i8] c"abc\00", [4 x i8]* %arr, align 1
  %0 = getelementptr inbounds [4 x i8], [4 x i8]* %arr, i32 0, i32 0
  store i8* %0, i8** %s, align 8
  store %User zeroinitializer, %User* %u, align 4
  %call.snprintf = call i32 (i8*, i64, i8*, ...) @snprintf(i8* null, i64 0, i8* getelementptr inbounds ([10 x i8], [10 x i8]* @str, i32 0, i32 0), i8* getelementptr inbounds ([4 x i8], [4 x i8]* @TYPEOF, i32 0, i32 0), i8* getelementptr inbounds ([5 x i8], [5 x i8]* @TYPEOF.1, i32 0, i32 0), i8* getelementptr inbounds ([5 x i8], [5 x i8]* @TYPEOF.2, i32 0, i32 0))
  store i32 %call.snprintf, i32* %format.size, align 4
  %"$format.size" = load i32, i32* %format.size, align 4
  %ADD = add i32 %"$format.size", 1
  %AS = sext i32 %ADD to i64
  %call.calloc = call i8* @calloc(i64 %AS, i64 sext (i32 ptrtoint (i8* getelementptr (i8, i8* null, i32 1) to i32) to i64))
  store i8* %call.calloc, i8** %format.text, align 8
  %"$format.text" = load i8*, i8** %format.text, align 8
  %call.snprintf1 = call i32 (i8*, i64, i8*, ...) @snprintf(i8* %"$format.text", i64 %AS, i8* getelementptr inbounds ([10 x i8], [10 x i8]* @str, i32 0, i32 0), i8* getelementptr inbounds ([4 x i8], [4 x i8]* @TYPEOF, i32 0, i32 0), i8* getelementptr inbounds ([5 x i8], [5 x i8]* @TYPEOF.1, i32 0, i32 0), i8* getelementptr inbounds ([5 x i8], [5 x i8]* @TYPEOF.2, i32 0, i32 0))
  %"$format.size2" = load i32, i32* %format.size, align 4
  %ADD3 = add i32 %"$format.size2", 1
  %"$format.text4" = load i8*, i8** %format.text, align 8
  %"$format.size5" = load i32, i32* %format.size, align 4
  %1 = insertvalue %String zeroinitializer, i8* %"$format.text4", 0
  %2 = insertvalue %String %1, i32 %"$format.size5", 1
  %3 = insertvalue %String %2, i32 %ADD3, 2
  call void @"io::out"(%String %3)
  ret i32 0
}

declare i8* @calloc(i64, i64)

declare i8* @memcpy(i8*, i8*, i64)

declare void @free(i8*)

declare i32 @snprintf(i8*, i64, i8*, ...)
```

## 004 — builtin: typeof on a ref and on a type name

```ura
struct User:
   count i32

fn main() i32:
   u User
   p &User = &u
   io::out($"{typeof(&p)} {typeof(i32)}\n")
   return 0
```

## 005 — builtin: sizeof folds to a constant

```ura
struct Pad:
   flag i8
   count i32

fn main() i32:
   x i32
   s i8[]
   p Pad
   return sizeof(x) + sizeof(s) + sizeof(p)
```

### llvm ir

```llvm
; ModuleID = 'ura'
source_filename = "ura"
target datalayout = "e-m:e-p270:32:32-p271:32:32-p272:64:64-i64:64-f80:128-n8:16:32:64-S128"
target triple = "x86_64-pc-linux-gnu"

%Pad = type { i8, i32 }

define i32 @main() {
entry:
  %x = alloca i32, align 4
  %s = alloca i8*, align 8
  %p = alloca %Pad, align 8
  store i32 0, i32* %x, align 4
  store i8* null, i8** %s, align 8
  store %Pad zeroinitializer, %Pad* %p, align 4
  ret i32 add (i32 add (i32 ptrtoint (i32* getelementptr (i32, i32* null, i32 1) to i32), i32 ptrtoint (i1** getelementptr (i1*, i1** null, i32 1) to i32)), i32 ptrtoint (%Pad* getelementptr (%Pad, %Pad* null, i32 1) to i32))
}
```

## 006 — builtin: typeof / sizeof on scalars

```ura
// stdout: typeof x = i32, sizeof x = 4
// stdout: typeof f = f32, sizeof f = 4
// stdout: sizeof i64 = 8
// expect: 0

fn main() i32:
    x i32   = 5
    f f32 = 1.5
    io::out($"typeof x = {typeof(x)}, sizeof x = {sizeof(x)}\n")
    io::out($"typeof f = {typeof(f)}, sizeof f = {sizeof(f)}\n")
    io::out($"sizeof i64 = {sizeof(1 as i64)}\n")
    return 0
```

## 007 — builtin: typeof / sizeof on an array

```ura
// stdout: typeof a = i32[], sizeof a = 8
// expect: 0

fn main() i32:
    a i32[] = [1, 2, 3]

    return 0
```

```ura
// stdout: i8[] bool

// expect: 0

fn main() i32:
    s char[] = "hello"
    b bool  = True
    io::out($"{typeof(s)} {typeof(b)}\n")
    io::out($"{sizeof("hello")} {sizeof(s)} {sizeof(b)}\n")
    return 0
```

## 012 — builtin: sizeof of a struct honours ABI padding

```ura
// stdout: 16
// expect: 0
struct Point:
    x i32
    y i64

fn main() i32:
    p Point
    io::out($"{sizeof(p)}\n")
    return 0
```

## 014 — cast: as casting + i64 / i16 via explicit cast

```ura
// stdout: 300
// stdout: 300
// stdout: 300
// stdout: 5
// expect: 0

fn main() i32:
    a i32   = 300
    big i64  = a as i64
    small i16 = a as i16
    c i32   = big as i32
    io::out($"{big}\n")
    io::out($"{small}\n")
    io::out($"{c}\n")
    io::out($"{5 as i64}\n")
    return 0
```

---

## 015 — cast: as converts between f32 and the integer types

```ura
// stdout: i32->f32 300.000000
// stdout: f32->i32 2 -2
// stdout: i64->f32 7.000000
// stdout: i16->f32 5.000000
// stdout: char->f32 65.000000 char->i32 65
// stdout: bool->f32 1.000000 bool->i32 1
// stdout: f32->i64 9
// stdout: round trip 4.000000
// expect: 0

fn main() i32:
    i i32   = 300
    f f32 = i as f32
    io::out($"i32->f32 {f}\n")
    io::out($"f32->i32 {2.9 as i32} {(0.0 - 2.9) as i32}\n")
    l i64  = 7 as i64
    io::out($"i64->f32 {l as f32}\n")
    s i16 = 5 as i16
    io::out($"i16->f32 {s as f32}\n")
    c char  = 'A'
    io::out($"char->f32 {c as f32} char->i32 {c as i32}\n")
    b bool  = True
    io::out($"bool->f32 {b as f32} bool->i32 {b as i32}\n")
    io::out($"f32->i64 {9.75 as i64}\n")
    io::out($"round trip {4.9 as i32 as f32}\n")
    return 0
```

## 016 — cast: char arithmetic and an expression cast back to char

```ura
// stdout: A 65 B
// stdout: 'd' - 'a' = 3
// stdout: spider
// expect: 0

fn main() i32:
    grade char = 'A'
    code  i32  = grade as i32
    // an expression cast back to char
    back  char = (code + 1) as char
    io::out($"{grade} {code} {back}\n")

    // arithmetic on two char literals
    diff i32 = 'd' as i32 - 'a' as i32
    io::out($"'d' - 'a' = {diff}\n")

    // an expression used as an array index
    roll i32 = 7
    names char[][] = ["orc", "spider", "dragon"]
    io::out($"{names[roll % 3]}\n")
    return 0
```

## 017 — cast: a struct cannot be cast to or from a scalar

```ura
// error: Cannot cast Point to i32

struct Point:
    x i32
    y i32

fn main() i32:
    p Point
    n i32 = p as i32
    io::out($"{n}\n")
    return 0
```

### stderr

```
error: test.ura:9:15 can't cast 'Point' to 'i32'
  |
9 |     n i32 = p as i32
  |               ^^
error: aborting due to 1 error
```

### status

```
1
```

```ura
// expect: 0

fn half() f32:
    return 0.5

fn main() i32:
    x f32 = 3.14
    io::out($"{x}\n")
    io::out($"{2.71}\n")
    io::out($"{half()}\n")
    y f32
    io::out($"{y}\n")
    return 0
```

## 020 — literal: f32 arithmetic + comparison, compound assignment

```ura
// stdout: 5.000000
// stdout: 1.000000
// stdout: 6.000000
// stdout: 1.500000
// stdout: True
// stdout: False
// stdout: 4.500000
// stdout: 9.000000
// expect: 0

fn main() i32:
    a f32 = 3.0
    b f32 = 2.0
    io::out($"{a + b}\n")
    io::out($"{a - b}\n")
    io::out($"{a * b}\n")
    io::out($"{a / b}\n")
    io::out($"{a > b}\n")
    io::out($"{a == b}\n")
    a += 1.5
    io::out($"{a}\n")
    a *= 2.0
    io::out($"{a}\n")
    return 0
```

---

## 021 — literal: f64 declaration, arithmetic and compound assignment

```ura
// stdout: 3.141593 2.000000
// stdout: 5.141593 1.141593 6.283185 1.570796
// stdout: True True True
// stdout: 6.000000
// stdout: 0.000000
// expect: 0

fn main() i32:
    d f64 = 3.141592653589793
    e f64 = 2.0
    io::out($"{d} {e}\n")
    io::out($"{d + e} {d - e} {d * e} {d / e}\n")
    io::out($"{d > e} {d == d} {d != e}\n")

    c f64 = 10.0
    c += 2.5
    c -= 0.5
    c *= 2.0
    c /= 4.0
    io::out($"{c}\n")

    // zero-init
    z f64
    io::out($"{z}\n")
    return 0
```


---

## 022 — literal: a f32 literal takes the type of its target

```ura
// stdout: False
// stdout: -5.000000 3.000000
// stdout: 0.500000
// expect: 0

fn half() f64:
    return 0.5

fn scale(x f64) f64:
    return x * 2.0

fn main() i32:
    // same digits, two different types
    d f64 = 3.141592653589793
    f f32  = 3.141592653589793
    // the f32 rounding is lossy, so widening it back does NOT equal d
    io::out($"{(f as f64) == d}\n")

    // an expression built only from literals adopts too, and unary minus
    // desugars to 0 - x, so its synthesised zero must be a f32
    io::out($"{scale(-2.5)} {scale(1.0 + 0.5)}\n")

    // inference at a return
    io::out($"{half()}\n")
    return 0
```

---

## 023 — literal: casts between f64, f32 and the integer types

```ura
// stdout: d->f32 3.750000  f->f64 1.500000
// stdout: d->i32 3  d->i64 3
// stdout: d->i16 3  d->char 
// stdout: i32->d 7.000000  bool->d 1.000000
// stdout: round trip 3.000000
// expect: 0

fn main() i32:
    d f64 = 3.75
    f f32  = 1.5
    io::out($"d->f32 {d as f32}  f->f64 {f as f64}\n")
    io::out($"d->i32 {d as i32}  d->i64 {d as i64}\n")
    io::out($"d->i16 {d as i16}  d->char {d as char}\n")
    io::out($"i32->d {7 as f64}  bool->d {True as f64}\n")
    io::out($"round trip {d as i32 as f64}\n")
    return 0
```

---

## 024 — literal: a return that does not match the function's type

```ura
// error: 'f' returns i32, but this returns f32

fn f() i32:
    return 1.5

fn main() i32:
    io::out($"{f()}\n")
    return 0
```

### stderr

```
error: test.ura:4:12 expected 'i32', found 'f64'
  |
4 |     return 1.5
  |            ^^^
error: aborting due to 1 error
```

### status

```
1
```

---

## 025 — literal: integer literals at the edge of each type's range

```ura
// stdout: 9223372036854775807 -9223372036854775807
// stdout: 2147483647 -2147483647
// stdout: 9007199254740993
// expect: 0

fn main() i32:
    i64_max i64 = 9223372036854775807
    i64_min i64 = -9223372036854775807
    i32_max i32 = 2147483647
    i32_min i32 = -2147483647
    exact   i64 = 9007199254740993

    io::out($"{i64_max} {i64_min}\n")
    io::out($"{i32_max} {i32_min}\n")
    io::out($"{exact}\n")
    return 0
```


---

## 026 — literal: an integer literal too wide for i32 keeps its width

```ura
// stdout: 9000000000
// stdout: 9000000000
// stdout: 9000000001
// stdout: 9000000001
// stdout: 18000000000
// stdout: 42 2147483647
// expect: 0

fn twice(n i64) i64:
    return n * 2

fn main() i32:
    with_target i64 = 9000000000
    io::out($"{with_target}\n")
    io::out($"{9000000000}\n")
    io::out($"{9000000000 + 1}\n")
    io::out($"{1 + 9000000000}\n")
    io::out($"{twice(9000000000)}\n")
    io::out($"{42} {2147483647}\n")
    return 0
```

## 027 — literal: a literal that does not fit its target type

```ura
// error: 300 does not fit in 'i8' (range -128 to 127); widen the type, or convert explicitly with 'as'

fn main() i32:
    small i8 = 300
    return 0
```

### stderr

```
error: test.ura:4:16 300 doesn't fit in i8
  |
4 |     small i8 = 300
  |                ^^^
help: i8 goes from -128 to 127
error: aborting due to 1 error
```

### status

```
1
```

## 028 — literal: a negative literal at the low edge still fits

```ura
// stdout: -128 -32768 255 65535
// expect: 0

fn main() i32:
    a i8  = -128
    b i16 = -32768
    c u8  = 255
    d u16 = 65535
    io::out($"{a as i32} {b} {c} {d}\n")
    return 0
```

## 029 — operator: bitwise & | ^ << >>

```ura
// stdout: 8
// stdout: 14
// stdout: 6
// stdout: 48
// stdout: 6
// expect: 0

fn main() i32:
    a i32 = 12
    b i32 = 10
    io::out($"{a & b}\n")
    io::out($"{a | b}\n")
    io::out($"{a ^ b}\n")
    io::out($"{a << 2}\n")
    io::out($"{a >> 1}\n")
    return 0
```

## 030 — operator: unary not / minus / bitwise-not

```ura
// stdout: -5
// stdout: False
// stdout: -6
// stdout: True
// stdout: 5
// expect: 0

fn main() i32:
    a i32 = 5
    t bool = True
    io::out($"{-a}\n")
    io::out($"{not t}\n")
    io::out($"{~a}\n")
    io::out($"{not (a < 3)}\n")
    io::out($"{-a + 10}\n")
    return 0
```

---

## 031 — operator: compound bitwise assignment

```ura
// stdout: and 8  or 15  xor 6
// stdout: shl 48  shr 12
// stdout: unsigned shr 2000000000
// stdout: signed shr -4
// expect: 0

fn main() i32:
    a i32 = 12
    a &= 10
    b i32 = 12
    b |= 3
    c i32 = 12
    c ^= 10
    io::out($"and {a}  or {b}  xor {c}\n")

    d i32 = 3
    d <<= 4
    e i32 = 48
    e >>= 2
    io::out($"shl {d}  shr {e}\n")

    // an unsigned right shift is logical, not arithmetic
    u u32 = 4000000000 as u32
    u >>= 1
    io::out($"unsigned shr {u}\n")

    // a signed one keeps the sign
    s i32 = 0 - 16
    s >>= 2
    io::out($"signed shr {s}\n")
    return 0
```

```llvm
@str.2 = private unnamed_addr constant [17 x i8] c"unsigned shr %u\0A\00", align 1
```

---

## 032 — operator: bitwise operator on f32 operands is rejected

```ura
// error: Bitwise and shift operators require integer operands

fn main() i32:
    x f32 = 1.5 & 2.0
    return 0
```

### stderr

```
error: test.ura:4:17 can't apply '&' to 'f64'
  |
4 |     x f32 = 1.5 & 2.0
  |                 ^
help: bitwise operators work on integers
error: aborting due to 1 error
```

### status

```
1
```

---

## 033 — operator: divide by zero prints file:line and exits 1

```ura
// stderr: runtime error: Division by zero
// expect: 1

fn main() i32:
    b i32 = 0
    a i32 = 10
    c i32 = a / b
    return 0
```

---

## 034 — operator: f32 division by zero traps (not inf)

```ura
// stderr: runtime error: Division by zero
// expect: 1

fn main() i32:
    b f32 = 0.0
    a f32 = 10.0
    c f32 = a / b
    return 0
```

---

## 035 — literal: f32 and f64 math, casts and printf

```ura
// stdout: 5.000000 1.000000 6.000000 1.500000
// stdout: 4.500000 9.000000 -9.000000
// stdout: 3.750000 3 7.000000 1.000000
// expect: 7
proto printf(fmt i8[], ...) i32

fn half() f32:
    return 0.5

fn main() i32:
    a f32 = 3.0
    b f32 = 2.0
    printf("%f %f %f %f\n", a + b, a - b, a * b, a / b)
    a += 1.5
    c f64 = 4.5
    c *= 2.0
    printf("%f %f %f\n", a, c, -c)
    d f64 = 3.75
    printf("%f %d %f %f\n", d, d as i32, 7 as f64, True as f64)
    if a > b and half() == 0.5:
        return 7
    return 0
```

```llvm
  %"$a7" = load float, float* %a, align 4

  store float %ADD8, float* %a, align 4

  store double 3.750000e+00, double* %d, align 8

  br label %if0

if0:                                              ; preds = %entry
  %"$a16" = load float, float* %a, align 4

endif:                                            ; preds = %if0

}
```
